Nobody applauded. They were too busy carrying blankets, finding families, and arguing about which door an ambulance could reach.","June heard the announcement over Ada's open microphone. She nearly laughed into her own camera. Then she saw the people waiting in the rehearsal room and remembered why she was recording.","The city's public statement still described the operation as a protective measure. The people arriving at the theater had not found the protection especially recognizable. A teenager clutched a bag containing one shoe, a charger, and a paper bracelet she had finally been allowed to remove. An older man kept asking whether his wife knew where he was. A nurse sat beside him and called the number he gave her.","June put the camera down.","\"Faces only with permission,\" she told the volunteer beside her. \"Names only when people want them used. We are making a record, not collecting exhibits.\"","The volunteer nodded. He had been a concertgoer when the evening began. He was now responsible for finding clean mugs. The progression seemed to have pleased him.","At the communication desk, Leda Reed was reading the latest list of arrivals. Amina stood beside her, one hand resting lightly on her shoulder. The theater that had once employed Leda had given her a talent for hearing a familiar voice through almost any amount of noise.","\"This way,\" she called to a frightened woman at the door. \"You are in the right place.\"","June watched her for a moment, then returned to the camera. There were kinds of courage that would never make a dramatic photograph. She was learning to leave room for them anyway.","Below the conservatory, Mara could hear only fragments of the work above ground. A name confirmed. A door opened. A vehicle sent to another address. The small reports made the locked room feel less complete.","Selene had stopped talking. She stood behind the desk, watching the terminal indicators change. Her authority was being reduced one person at a time.","\"You cannot move them all,\" she said.","\"Then we will move the next one,\" Mara replied.","Cassian had found a maintenance panel near the sealed partition. It did not control the patients' devices. It controlled the room's ventilation and the local door release. He asked Ivo to identify the labels before attempting anything. Mara watched Selene's face as he read them aloud.","At the words manual release, Selene's hand tightened.","Mara smiled. \"There you are.\"","\"That panel is not intended for public use.\"","\"Neither was your emergency.\"","The radio broke into static. For a second every voice vanished. Mara felt the loss physically, like a door closing inside her chest.","Then Ivo returned, sounding intensely offended. Someone had tried to flood the observation line with repeated status messages. The old link had too little capacity to carry the traffic. He had disconnected its ordinary terminal feed and retained only the narrow voice channel.","\"No photographs, no long files,\" he said. \"Short messages. One person at a time.\"","\"A devastating restriction on June,\" Cassian observed.","\"I heard that,\" June said at once.","Mara laughed, and heard relief in the small answering sounds from the other end. The joke had done what a command could not: reminded them that the voices belonged to people they knew.","Tessa asked for the terminal list. Mara read the identifiers slowly. Noor interrupted once to correct a location. Two devices had been moved since the tower's last report. The old map was useful. It was not the territory.","\"One of those rooms has an independent attendant,\" Noor said. \"We are trying the direct line.\"","Selene leaned across the desk. \"The attendants know their obligations.\"","\"So do we,\" Mara said.","Outside, rain struck the glass roof. Through it came a distant sound that did not belong to the machinery: music. Someone at the opera house had opened the street doors. A brass player was testing a few notes, then stopping to let a vehicle through. The sound was rough, cheerful, and entirely unscheduled.","Mara imagined the conductor's face. It helped.","On June's side of the city, the public video feed opened with an empty chair. A title card identified the date, the place, and what the camera could and could not show. No patient's private record appeared. Instead, the screen displayed the company's own public statement beside the maintenance schedule recovered from the tower.","June read the statement aloud. Then she read the sections that explained who still had to be in contact with a terminal for the operation to affect them. She did not claim that everyone in Asterfall was about to lose their memory. The actual danger required no enlargement.","The first viewer called it a hoax. The second asked where a missing relative had been taken. The third supplied an address.","Messages began to arrive faster than the volunteers could verify them.","Ada brought a second table. A librarian devised a simple system for keeping confirmed reports separate from guesses. Someone who had been studying the history of public health offered to check the facility names against older directories. A bus driver said he could get through the service road if the theater could supply a passenger list.","The city did not become uniformly brave. Some people stayed home. Some argued. Some promoted a version of events that suited a pre-existing grievance. But enough people chose a concrete, useful task to make the next one possible.","Below the garden, Selene watched the first independent report appear on her own screen.","\"You are destroying confidence,\" she said.","Mara looked at the names beside the terminal indicators. Another changed to clear.","\"I am giving it somewhere else to go.\"","Cassian found the manual release behind a narrow cover. The door opened with a reluctant mechanical sound. On the far side stood the young guard from the stairs. He held his radio in one hand and a ring of keys in the other.","\"The lower room,\" he said. \"There are still people there. The attendant won't open it.\"","Mara turned towards Selene. \"You can end this now.\"","For the first time, the woman's perfect composure broke. It was only a glimpse: rage, disbelief, and beneath them something Mara recognized as fear.","Selene's hand moved towards the desk.","Cassian caught her wrist before she reached the key. He did not twist it. He simply held it away from the controls while Mara stepped between them and the terminal screen.","\"No more unsupervised decisions,\" he said.","The clock passed nineteen minutes.","Over the old observation line came Noor's voice.","\"We have the address. We need those keys.\""],"questions":[{"type":"论证结构","stem":"Why does June identify what the public camera can actually show?","options":["She believes an empty chair proves that no one is in chair?</strong></p><div class=\"options\"><button type=\"button\" data-option=\"0\">A. She asks the public broadcast to diagnose Mira&#x27;s condition.</button><button type=\"button\" data-option=\"1\">B. She treats reluctance as proof that Mira has knowingly supported Selene.</button><button type=\"button\" data-option=\"2\">C. She guarantees that no adverse consequence of any kind is possible.</button><button type=\"button\" data-option=\"3\">D. She explains the confirmed information and remaining checks while respecting Mira&#x27;s concern.</button></div><div class=\"feedback\" hidden=\"hidden\"></div></div></section><nav class=\"chapter-nav\" aria-label=\"Chapter navigation\"><button data-go=\"88\">← Previous</button><button data-go=\"90\">Next →</button></nav></article>"},{"id":"v08c07","title":"Proof with the Power Off","volume":"卷八｜他们记得的世界","anchor":"chapter-0091","paragraphs":["\"Central confirmation,\" Selene said. \"You have my ring. You do not have the whole institution.\"","Mara read the message again. She disliked how reasonable it looked. A system could refuse to stop in impeccably polite language.","Tessa asked for the current terminal state. Mara supplied it. Ivo compared the result with the independent confirmations from the rooms. Every known participant had been removed from contact. The clinical part of the threat had been broken by people opening doors, making calls, and refusing to leave frightened strangers behind.","The remaining clock governed the scheduled records operation. Some files would be overwritten, including material they had not yet examined. Mara looked at the little frozen image of her mother on the side screen.","Selene followed her gaze.","\"You can still preserve what you came for.\"","Mara felt Cassian move beside her. He had seen old recordings of the accident too. The offer was aimed at both of them, and neither could dismiss it as something they did not want.","\"Tell Tessa what the system permits,\" Mara said.","Selene looked surprised. \"You are asking me for help?\"","\"I am giving you another opportunity to be useful.\"","The words came out with a calm Mara did not feel. She wanted to shake the woman. She wanted to demand every stolen minute back, as if the right degree of anger could make a machine return a life intact. Instead she held the radio where Selene could see it.","For several seconds Selene said nothing. Then she began to explain the storage arrangement.","Tessa interrupted almost immediately. \"That is the trial buffer. I asked about the audit storage.\"","A pause.","Ivo made a small, satisfied noise. \"Separate units. As in the older plan.\"","They could not rescue every file by sending it through the observation line. They did not try. The plan was less elegant: preserve the local audit material already accessible, isolate the archive from further scheduled changes, and leave the equipment itself for independent examination. Some recent buffers might be lost in the interruption. What remained would have to be interpreted, not worshipped as a flawless picture of reality.","Mara looked at her mother's image again. Elena would still have gaps. So would Cassian. There would be questions no recovered file could answer for them.","She set the screen aside.","\"Do the safe preservation,\" she said. \"We are not putting anyone back in a chair to make the archive more complete.\"","Selene's expression changed. Mara could not tell whether the woman thought her foolish or had recognized a decision she herself had once refused to make.","The young guard returned with two independent technicians. One had been working with Varr; the other carried the sealed materials requested by the court. They had come through the route the volunteers kept open, guided by the same imperfect plan that had led Mara downstairs.","Cassian moved away from the desk. The technicians identified themselves on June's camera, recorded what they found, and asked Tessa to remain on the line. A procedure that had looked like a private contest between Mara and Selene became work other people could witness.","That, Mara thought, was the first part of the victory she actually trusted.","On the screen, a copying indicator appeared. A child woke and asked whether the noise outside meant there would be no school. In the opera house, a nurse found a missing coat under a chair. Someone in the kitchen burned the first batch of toast and began another.","Mara had never heard such a glorious collection of ordinary failures.","She stood at the conservatory's service entrance while the independent team checked the final terminal report. The rain had stopped. Water dripped from the roof in slow, irregular drops. Cassian waited beside her with a blanket one of the volunteers had insisted he take.","\"You look tired,\" she said.","\"A speculative judgment.\"","\"I have a promising sample.\"","He wrapped the blanket around her shoulders instead. She opened her mouth to object, then saw that he had kept half of it. They stood under it together, close enough to stop pretending either was doing the other a favor.","Behind them, Selene was led towards a waiting vehicle. She had not become less intelligent, less controlled, or suddenly easy to understand. She had become a person required to answer questions she could not choose. Mara watched until the door closed.","There would be hearings, examination of the records, conflicting accounts, and arguments about responsibility. The night had not completed that work. It had made the work possible.","Mara turned away.","At the opera house, the little brass ensemble was still playing. It had acquired a violinist, a drummer, and a child who rang a triangle whenever she judged the music in need of encouragement. The conductor had abandoned the attempt to control her and appeared happier for it.","The crowd outside parted when Mara and Cassian arrived. She was prepared for questions. She was not prepared for the port workers to begin applauding.","Leah stood at the front. Anika was beside her. Rook raised the rubber goose above her head like an entirely unsuitable royal emblem.","\"Speech!\" someone shouted.","\"No,\" Mara said.","The answer produced more applause.","She looked helplessly at Cassian. His expression was innocent in a way she had learned to distrust.","\"You could tell them about the toast,\" he suggested.","\"I could tell them about your volleyball career.\"","He took her hand. \"A generous offer.\"","Then the door opened, and Elena came out with Sister Agnes on one side and Noor on the other. She should not have been standing for long. Everyone knew that. For once nobody attempted to improve the moment by saying it.","Mara crossed the space between them.","Her mother smelled of hospital soap and the tea she always forgot to finish. Mara put her arms around her carefully, then less carefully, because Elena was holding her with a strength that had nothing to do with how far she could walk.","\"You came back,\" Elena said.","It was a simple sentence. It contained everything Mara had been unable to promise herself for years.","\"I brought him,\" she said into her mother's shoulder.","Elena looked at Cassian, who had stopped a respectful distance away. She held out a hand. He took it.","\"I have been hearing about you,\" she said.","Mara lifted her head. \"From whom?\"","\"Several reliable sources.\"","Noor looked elsewhere. June became very interested in her camera. Rowan, arriving behind them with a box of food, did not bother to conceal his smile.","\"I shall require the original reports,\" Mara said.","Elena touched her cheek. \"Tomorrow.\"","For once Mara accepted the delay.","Inside the theater, Leda was helping a family find a quiet room. Ada had fallen asleep across two chairs with a clipboard on her lap. Orr was carrying another box, complaining to nobody in particular that a conservation fellowship had not prepared him for this much practical furniture. Mrs. Wren had discovered the kitchen and was correcting its organization with the authority of a woman who had never needed an official title.","Ivo sat on the stage steps while Anika examined the scrape on his hand. He was insisting that it was trivial. She was telling him that trivial injuries did not become less trivial when ignored. Mara recognized the exchange and decided, mercifully, not to comment.","June approached with her notebook closed.","\"One question,\" she said.","\"You have used that phrase before.\"","\"Would you like the camera off?\"","Mara looked at the room. At the blankets, the cups, the people who had arrived frightened and were beginning to look annoyed about small things again. She thought of the missing records, the files that would not give back a childhood, the long work of making an institution safe enough to deserve confidence.","\"Keep it off for a moment,\" she said.","June put the camera down.","Mara sat on the edge of the stage. Cassian sat beside her. Someone gave them a plate, then another, as though feeding them might be a satisfactory way to express feelings too large for a tired room.","\"We were supposed to have dinner,\" he said.","\"This is not a respectable location.\"","\"You specified that.\"","She leaned her head against his shoulder. Across the room, a child struck the triangle once. The little note hung in the air after the other instruments had stopped.","Mara began to laugh. Then she cried, quietly and without grace, while Cassian held her hand and nobody asked her to explain.","Above the theater entrance, the old clock marked the hour. It had no power over them except the ordinary, difficult power of time continuing.","By morning, there would be work.","For now, they were home."],"questions":[{"type":"主旨","stem":"The chapter's
